\displaystyle\approx\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}}\left[\dfrac{(1-R^{2})(1-|x|^{2})}{R^{2}|x-z_{1}^{\star}|^{2}}\right]^{c\alpha}\left[\dfrac{(1-R^{2})(1-|x|^{2})}{R^{2}|x-z_{2}^{\star}|^{2}}\right]^{c\beta(p-1)}\dfrac{1}{(1-|x|^{2})^{n}}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\approx\dfrac{(1-R^{2})^{c(\alpha+\beta(p-1))}}{R^{2c(\alpha+\beta(p-1))}}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\dfrac{(1-|x|^{2})^{c(\alpha+\beta(p-1))-n}}{|x-z_{1}^{\star}|^{2c\alpha}|x-z_{2}^{\star}|^{2c\beta(p-1)}}\,{\rm d}x.

Since
|x-z_{1}^{\star}|\approx 1
in
\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}
as
R\rightarrow 1
we therefore can neglect the term
|x-z_{1}^{*}|.
Hence

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}}U^{\alpha}V^{\beta(p-1)}\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}

\displaystyle\approx(1-R^{2})^{c(\alpha+\beta(p-1))}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}}\dfrac{(1-|x|^{2})^{c(\alpha+\beta(p-1))-n}}{|x-z_{2}^{\star}|^{2c\beta(p-1)}}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\approx(1-R^{2})^{c(\alpha+\beta(p-1))}\begin{cases}1,\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mbox{if}\ \alpha>\beta(p-1),\\
d(0,z_{2}),\ \ \ \ \mbox{if}\ \alpha=\beta(p-1),\end{cases}

which is precisely (
9.14
) and hence the proposition follows.
∎

The next result is devoted to finding a lower bound on
\|f\|_{H^{-1}},
where
f=(U+V)^{p}-U^{p}-V^{p}\geq 0
and
1<p\leq 2.
In particular, we prove the following lemma

Lemma 9.5
.

Let
z_{1}=-Re_{1}
and
z_{2}=Re_{1}.
Then

(9.15)

\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\gtrsim\begin{cases}(d(z_{1},z_{2}))^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-c(n,\lambda)d(z_{1},z_{2})},\quad\mbox{if}\ p=2,\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pte^{-\frac{c(n,\lambda)p}{2}d(z_{1},z_{2})}\qquad\qquad\qquad\mbox{if}\ 1<p<2.\end{cases}

Proof.

Let us first consider the special case
p=2
for which
f
simplifies to
f=2UV.
Fix any
\alpha\geq 2.
Then we have

(9.16)

\displaystyle d(z_{1},z_{2})e^{-c(n,\lambda)\alpha d(z_{1},z_{2})}\approx\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}U^{\alpha}V^{\alpha}\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\approx\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}fU^{\alpha-1}V^{\alpha-1}\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\leq\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\|U^{\alpha-1}V^{\alpha-1}\|_{H^{1}}.

A direct computation of the
H^{1}
norm yields

\displaystyle\|U^{\alpha-1}V^{\alpha-1}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle=\|\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}(U^{\alpha-1}V^{\alpha-1})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}|U^{\alpha-1}(\alpha-1)(\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}V)V^{\alpha-2}\,+\,V^{\alpha-1}(\alpha-1)U^{\alpha-2}\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}U|^{2}\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(9.17)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}U^{2(\alpha-1)}V^{2(\alpha-1)}\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\approx e^{-c(n,\lambda)(\alpha-1)d(z_{1},z_{2})}(d(z_{1},z_{2}))^{\frac{1}{2}},

where in the last line we have used
\|\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\mathcal{U}\|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\lesssim\mathcal{U}.

Now substituting (
9.4
) into (
9.16
) we obtain the desired result.

Next, we shall consider the case
1<p<2.
We use Green function estimates of
-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}
in the hyperbolic space. Let
G(x,y)
be the
Green function of
-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}
and is given by
G(x,y):=G(d(x,y)):=\int_{d(x,y)}^{\infty}(\sinh s)^{-(n-1)}\,{\rm d}s.
Then
G
satisfies

G(x,y)\geq C_{n}\,e^{-(n-1)d(x,y)},

whenever
d(x,y)\geq 1
and
C_{n}
is a dimensional constant. Let us define

u(x):=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}G(x,y)\,f(y)\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}(y).

Then
u
satisfies,
-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u=f
in
\mathbb{B}^{n}.
Further, multiplying this equation by
u
and integration by parts gives

\|\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}f\,u\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\leq\,\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\|\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u\|_{L^{2}},

and hence we obtain
\|\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|f\|_{H^{-1}}.
Then the Poincaré-Sobolev inequality gives us

\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\geq\|\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u\|_{L^{2}}\geq\|u\|_{L^{p+1}}.

Our aim is now to derive a point-wise lower bound of
u.
Recall from subsection
9.1
,

(9.18)

f\approx\begin{cases}UV^{p-1}\quad\mbox{in}\ \mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}\\
U^{p-1}V\quad\mbox{in}\ \mathbb{B}^{n}_{-}.\end{cases}

Let us denote
\Omega=\{x\in\mathbb{B}^{n}:x_{1}<-\frac{1}{2}\},
then for
x\in\Omega
and
y\in\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+},

d(x,y)\gtrsim 1,
and hence

\displaystyle u(x)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}G(x,y)\,f(y)\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}(y)\gtrsim\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}}G(x,y)\ UV^{p-1}(y)\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}(y)

\displaystyle\approx\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}}e^{-(n-1)d(x,y)}e^{-c(n,\lambda)d(y,z_{1})}e^{-c(n,\lambda)(p-1)d(y,z_{2})}\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}(y)

\displaystyle\approx\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}}\left[\left(\dfrac{(1-|x|^{2})(1-|y|^{2})}{|x|^{2}|y-x^{\star}|^{2}}\right)^{n-1}\left(\dfrac{(1-R^{2})(1-|y|^{2})}{R^{2}|y-z_{1}^{\star}|^{2}}\right)^{c(n,\lambda)}\times\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \ \ \left.\left(\dfrac{(1-R^{2})(1-|y|^{2})}{R^{2}|y-z_{2}^{\star}|^{2}}\right)^{c(n,\lambda)(p-1)}(1-|y|^{2})^{-n}\right]\,{\rm d}y

(9.19)

\displaystyle\approx(1-R^{2})^{c(n,\lambda)p}\dfrac{(1-|x|^{2})^{n-1}}{|x|^{2(n-1)}}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}}\dfrac{(1-|y|^{2})^{c(n,\lambda)p-1}}{|y-z_{2}^{\star}|^{2c(n,\lambda)(p-1)}|y-x^{\star}|^{2(n-1)}}\,{\rm d}y.

In the last line we have used the fact that
|y-z_{1}^{\star}|\approx 1
if
y\in\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}.
Also by our assumption
|y-x^{\star}|\approx 1,
whenever
y\in\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}

and
x\in\Omega.
Moreover,
2c(n,\lambda)(p-1)-c(n,\lambda)p+1=c(n,\lambda)p-2c(n,\lambda)+1=c(n,\lambda)(p-2)+1<n
and hence

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}}\dfrac{(1-|y|^{2})^{c(n,\lambda)p-1}}{|y-z_{2}^{\star}|^{2c(n,\lambda)(p-1)}}\,{\rm d}y\gtrsim 1

Therefore we deduce from (
9.4
) that

\displaystyle u(x)\gtrsim(1-R^{2})^{c(n,\lambda)p}(1-|x|^{2})^{n-1}\quad\mbox{in}\ \Omega,

and consequently,

\displaystyle\|u\|_{L^{p+1}}\gtrsim(1-R^{2})^{c(n,\lambda)p}\left(\int_{\Omega}(1-|x|^{2})^{(n-1)(p+1)-n}\,{\rm d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{p+1}}\approx(1-R^{2})^{c(n,\lambda)p}.

Since
e^{-\frac{d(z_{1},z_{2})}{2}}\approx(1-R^{2}),
the proof of the proposition has been completed.
∎

As a consequence of the above results we prove

Proposition 9.3
.

Let
\Psi
satisfies (
9.7
). Then there exists a constant
\alpha_{0}>0
depending on
n,\lambda
and
p
such that

(9.20)

\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}f\,\Psi\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\right|\lesssim\begin{cases}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}^{1+\alpha_{0}},\quad\ \ \ \ \qquad\quad\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mbox{if}\ 1<p<2,\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\Big|\ln\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\Big|^{-\frac{1}{2}},\quad\ \ \ \ \mbox{if}\ p=2.\end{cases}

Proof.

We first consider
1<p<2.
Let
\alpha>0
be the small quantity determined by Proposition
9.1
. We denote
W=U+V,
then
W\approx V
(respectively,
U
) in
\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}
(respectively,
\mathbb{B}^{n}_{-}
). By Cauchy-Schwartz inequality we deduce

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}f\,\Psi\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}}UV^{p-1}|\Psi|\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\,+\,\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{-}}U^{p-1}V|\Psi|\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}

\displaystyle\approx\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}}UV^{p-1+\alpha}\frac{|\Psi|}{W^{\alpha}}\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\,+\,\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{-}}U^{p-1+\alpha}V\frac{|\Psi|}{W^{\alpha}}\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}}U^{2}V^{2(p-1+\alpha)}\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,+\,\left(\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{-}}U^{2(p-1+\alpha)}V^{2}\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\approx e^{-c(n,\lambda)\frac{2+2(p-1+\alpha)}{4}d(z_{1},z_{2})}\ \ \ \mbox{if $p-1+\alpha<1$}

(9.21)

\displaystyle\approx\left(e^{-c(n,\lambda)\frac{p}{2}d(z_{1},z_{2})}\right)^{\frac{p+\alpha}{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{-1}}^{1+\alpha_{0}},

where
\alpha_{0}=\frac{\alpha}{p}.
Note that if
p-1+\alpha\geq 1,
then (
9.4
) is still valid for any
\alpha_{0}<\frac{2-p}{p}.
The case,
p=2
can be tackled as follows

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}f\,\Psi\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\right|\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}UV|\Psi|\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}}U^{2}V^{2(1+\alpha)}\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{-}}U^{2(1+\alpha)}V^{2}\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}U^{2}V^{2(1+\alpha)}\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(9.22)

\displaystyle\approx e^{-c(n,\lambda)d(z_{1},z_{2})}

By Lemma
9.5
and using the bound
\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}}
we get
|\ln\|f\|_{H^{-1}}|\approx d(z_{1},z_{2})
and hence
e^{-c(n,\lambda)d(z_{1},z_{2})}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{-1}}|\ln\|f\|_{H^{-1}}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}.
This completes the proof of the lemma.

∎

Based on the above estimates, we can now follow the ideas of Figalli and Glaudo
[
28
]
and complete the proof of sharpness of the stability estimate of Theorem
1.1
. There are a few technical points that need to be addressed in the case of the hyperbolic space, however, the main arguments are almost along the lines of
[
28
]
. For the reader’s convenience, we plan to provide as many details as possible, so that the article can be read without prior consultation of the Euclidean case.

9.5.
The subspaces
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{F}
are close

We recall the definition of generalized Hausedorff distance between two subspaces of a Hilbert space
H

[
42
,
28
]
.

Definition 9.1
.

Let
X
be a Hilbert space and let
\|\cdot\|_{X}
be the associated norm. Let
E,F
be two subspaces of
X.
The distance
d_{X}(E,F)
between them is defined as

\displaystyle d_{X}(E,F)=d_{H}(\overline{B_{X}(0,1)}\cap E,\overline{B_{X}(0,1)}\cap F).

where
d_{H}
is the Hausedorff distance defined by

\displaystyle d_{H}(A,B)=\inf\{r>0\ |\ A\subset B+\overline{B_{X}(0,r)},\ \mbox{and}\ B\subset A+\overline{B_{X}(0,r)}\},

and
B_{X}(0,r)
denotes the open ball in
X
of radius
r.
It is easy to verify that the Hausedorff distance satisfies the scaling property
d_{H}(\kappa A,\kappa B)=\kappa\ d_{H}(A,B),
for
\kappa>0.

The main properties of
d_{X}
used in this article are mentioned in the next few bullet points

•

For every subspaces
E,F
of
X
it holds
d_{X}(E,F)\leq 1,
and if two subspaces have different dimensions then
d_{X}(E,F)=1.

•

If
E
and
F
are two closed subspaces of
X,
then
d_{X}(E,F)
is given by

\displaystyle d_{X}(E,F)=\sup_{e\in E\backslash\{0\}}\frac{\|e-\pi_{F}(e)\|_{X}}{\|e\|_{X}}=\sup_{f\in F\backslash\{0\}}\frac{\|f-\pi_{E}(f)\|_{X}}{\|f\|_{X}},

where
\pi_{E}
and
\pi_{F}
are the projection operators onto
E
and
F
respectively. Hence as a corollary

(9.23)

\displaystyle\|e-\pi_{F}(e)\|_{X}\leq d_{X}(E,F)\|e\|_{X},\ \ \|f-\pi_{E}(f)\|_{X}\leq d(E,F)\|f\|_{F}

holds for every
e\in E
and
f\in F.

•

d_{X}
is invariant under orthogonal complement, i.e.,

\displaystyle d_{X}(E,F)=d_{X}(E^{\bot},F^{\bot}).

In this section we assume
X=L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
and
E=\mathcal{E},F=\mathcal{F}
where
\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}
are defined in section
9.1
. The main purpose of this section is to prove that
d_{X}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})=o(1),
which would ensure
\mbox{dim}\ \mathcal{E}=\mbox{dim}\ \mathcal{F}.

Lemma 9.6
.

Let
X=L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n}),
then

d_{X}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})=o(1)
as
R\uparrow 1.
In particular,

\displaystyle\mbox{dim}\ \mathcal{E}=\mbox{dim}\ \mathcal{F}=2(n+1).

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

Step 1:
For any
\psi\in B_{\mathcal{F}}
there exists
\psi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}
such that

\displaystyle\|\psi-\psi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}=o(1),\ \ \mbox{as}\ R\uparrow 1.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\psi
satisfies

\displaystyle-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\psi-\lambda\psi=\mu U^{p-1}\psi,\ \ \ \mbox{where}\ \ \mu=1\ \mbox{or}\ p.

The idea is to project
\psi
onto
\mathcal{E}.
We rewrite the equation satisfied by
\psi
as follows

\displaystyle-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\psi-\lambda\psi-\mu(U+V)^{p-1}\psi=\mu\left(U^{p-1}-(U+V)^{p-1}\right)\psi=:\ f.

We write
\psi=\sum_{k}\alpha_{k}\psi_{k},
where
\{\psi_{k}\}
is the
L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}
-orthonormal basis consisting of eigenvectors of
(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)/((U+V)^{p-1}).
Then by Lemma
A.5
(a) we get

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\alpha_{k}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\mu}{\lambda_{k}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\|(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)^{-1}f\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}|U^{p-1}-(U+V)^{p-1}|^{2}\psi^{2}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}|U^{p-1}-(U+V)^{p-1}|^{\frac{2(p+1)}{p-1}}\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p+1}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}|\psi|^{p+1}\right)^{\frac{2}{p+1}}

\displaystyle=o(1),\ \ \mbox{as}\ \ R\uparrow 1.

We define
\psi^{\prime}=\pi_{\mathcal{E}}(\psi),
the projection of
\psi
on to the subspace

\mathcal{E}.
Then

\displaystyle\|\psi-\psi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda_{k}\geq p(1-\epsilon)^{-1}}\alpha_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\sum_{\lambda_{k}\geq p(1-\epsilon)^{-1}}\alpha_{k}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\mu}{\lambda_{k}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=o(1),\ \ \mbox{as}\ \ R\uparrow 1.

Step 2:
For any
\psi\in B_{\mathcal{E}},
there exists
\psi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}

such that

\displaystyle\|\psi-\psi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}=o(1),\ \ \mbox{as}\ \ R\uparrow 1.

The idea is to localize
\psi
around the bubbles
U
and
V
respectively and approximate the localized function by the sum of elements of
\mathcal{F}.
Here recall

\mathcal{F}
is composed of eigenvectors of
(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)/U^{p-1}
and
(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)/V^{p-1}
with eigenvalues
1
and
p
respectively.

Let
\eta
be a smooth cutoff function identically
1
around
-Re_{1}.
We will decide
\eta
later. Set
\varphi_{U}=\psi\eta
and we write
\varphi_{U}=\sum_{k}\beta_{k}\varphi_{k},
where

\{\varphi_{k}\}
is an orthonormal basis of
L^{2}_{U^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
consisting of eigenvectors of
(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)/U^{p-1}.
Here for clarity, we remark that orthonormal with respect to the
L^{2}_{U^{p-1}}
inner product, and we denote the eigenvalues by
\{\tilde{\lambda}_{k}\}
. By Lemma
A.5
(a), denoting
f=(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda-\mu U^{p-1})\varphi_{U}
we get

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\beta_{k}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\mu}{\tilde{\lambda}_{k}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\|(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)^{-1}f\|_{L^{2}_{U^{p-1}}}^{2}

(9.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{2}=\|(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda-\mu U^{p-1})\varphi_{U}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{2}.

Since
(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda-\mu(U+V)^{p-1})\psi=0,
where
\mu<p(1-\epsilon)^{-1},

we compute

\displaystyle(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda-\mu U^{p-1})\varphi_{U}

\displaystyle=(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda-\mu U^{p-1})(\psi\eta)

\displaystyle=(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\eta)\psi-2\langle\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\psi,\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\eta\rangle_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}+\mu((U+V)^{p-1}-U^{p-1})\psi\eta.

Let
d=d(-Re_{1},Re_{1}).
Let
\eta
be the cut off function as in Lemma
6.1
, such that
\eta\equiv 1
on
B(-Re_{1},\frac{d}{4})
and support
\eta
contained in
B(-Re_{1},\frac{d}{2})
so that

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\|\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}=o(1).

Then we have supp
\varphi_{U}\subset\mathbb{B}^{n}_{-}
and

\displaystyle\|(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda-\mu U^{p-1})\varphi_{U}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\psi\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}+2\|\langle\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\psi,\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\eta\rangle_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +\|\mu((U+V)^{p-1}-U^{p-1})\psi\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}

(9.25)

\displaystyle=o(1),\ \ \mbox{as}\ \ R\uparrow 1.

From (
9.5
) and (
9.5
) we get
\sum_{k}\beta_{k}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\mu}{\tilde{\lambda}_{k}}\right)^{2}=o(1)
as
R\uparrow 1.
We set
\psi_{U}=\sum_{\tilde{\lambda}_{k}<p(1-\epsilon)^{-1}}\beta_{k}\varphi_{k}\in\mathcal{F},
then

\displaystyle\|\psi_{U}-\varphi_{U}\|_{L^{2}_{U^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}=\sum_{\tilde{\lambda}_{k}\geq p(1-\epsilon)^{-1}}\beta_{k}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\sum_{\tilde{\lambda}_{k}\geq p(1-\epsilon)^{-1}}\beta_{k}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\mu}{\tilde{\lambda}_{k}}\right)^{2}=o(1),\ \ \mbox{as}\ \ R\uparrow 1.

Similarly, we find
\varphi_{V}
such that supp
(\varphi_{V})\subset\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}
and
\psi_{V}\in\mathcal{F}
such that

\displaystyle\|\psi_{V}-\varphi_{V}\|_{L^{2}_{V^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}=o(1),\ \ \mbox{as}\ \ R\uparrow 1.

We recall that
U+V\approx U
in
\mathbb{B}^{n}_{-}
and
\approx V
in
\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}.
Also, recall that

\psi_{U}
is an element of
\mathcal{F}_{U},

|\psi_{U}|\lesssim U
holds.
With this information at hand, we refine the previous estimates as follows

\displaystyle\|\psi_{U}-\varphi_{U}\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\psi_{U}-\varphi_{U}\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n}_{-})}+\|\psi_{U}\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\psi_{U}-\varphi_{U}\|_{L^{2}_{U^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n}_{-})}+\|\psi_{U}\|_{L^{2}_{V^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+})}

\displaystyle=o(1),\ \ \mbox{as}\ \ R\uparrow 1.

In the last line, we have used

\displaystyle\|\psi_{U}\|_{L^{2}_{V^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+})}^{2}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{+}}U^{2}V^{p-1}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}=o(1)\ \ \mbox{as}\ \ R\uparrow 1.

Similarly, we get

\displaystyle\|\psi_{V}-\varphi_{V}\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}=o(1),\ \ \mbox{as}\ \ R\uparrow 1.

We set
\psi^{\prime}=\psi_{U}+\psi_{V},
then

\displaystyle\|\psi-\psi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi_{U}-\psi_{U}\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\|\varphi_{V}-\psi_{V}\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +\|\psi-\varphi_{U}-\varphi_{V}\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}

\displaystyle=o(1)+\|\psi-\varphi_{U}-\varphi_{V}\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})},\ \ \mbox{as}\ \ R\uparrow 1.

Since
\varphi_{U}=\psi
on
B(-Re_{1},\frac{d}{4})
and
\varphi_{V}=\psi
on
B(Re_{1},\frac{d}{4})
we have

\displaystyle\|\psi-\varphi_{U}-\varphi_{V}\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash(B(-Re_{1},\frac{d}{4})\cup B(Re_{1},\frac{d}{4}))}\psi^{2}(U+V)^{p-1}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim o(1)\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\psi^{2}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}

\displaystyle=o(1),\ \ \mbox{as}\ \ R\uparrow 1.

This completes the proof.
∎

As a corollary, we have the following lemma

Lemma 9.7
.

Let
f
and
\tilde{f}
be defined as in (
9.1
) and (
9.2
) respectively and
R\approx 1.

(a)

If
p=2,
then

\displaystyle\|f-\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\frac{p+1}{p}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\Big|\ln\|f\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\Big|^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(b)

If
1<p<2,
then there exists
\alpha_{0}>0
depending on
n,\lambda
and
p
such that

\displaystyle\|f-\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\frac{p+1}{p}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{1+\alpha_{0}}.

In particular,
\|f-\tilde{f}\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|f-\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\frac{p+1}{p}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}=o(\|f\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}).

Proof.

By definition

\displaystyle f-\tilde{f}

\displaystyle=(U+V)^{p-1}\left[\frac{f}{(U+V)^{p-1}}-\pi_{\mathcal{E}}\left(\frac{f}{(U+V)^{p-1}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=(U+V)^{p-1}\sum_{\psi\in B_{\mathcal{E}}}\langle\frac{f}{(U+V)^{p-1}},\psi\rangle_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\psi

\displaystyle=(U+V)^{p-1}\sum_{\psi\in B_{\mathcal{E}}}\langle f,\psi\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\psi.

Hence,

\displaystyle\|f-\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\frac{p+1}{p}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\psi\in B_{\mathcal{E}}}|\langle f,\psi\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}|\|\psi(U+V)^{p-1}\|_{L^{\frac{p+1}{p}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\psi\in B_{\mathcal{E}}}|\langle f,\psi\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}|\|\psi\|_{L^{p+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\|U+V\|_{L^{p+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\psi\in B_{\mathcal{E}}}|\langle f,\psi\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}|.

By Proposition
9.3
, the lemma follows.
∎

Thanks to (
9.2
), Lemma
9.7
and (
9.6
) we have the following

Lemma 9.8
.

If
R\approx 1
, then

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u+\lambda u+|u|^{p-1}u\|_{L^{\frac{p+1}{p}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\begin{cases}\|\rho\|_{\lambda}|\ln\|\rho\|_{\lambda}|^{-\frac{1}{2}},\ \ \ \ \mbox{if}\ p=2,\\
\|\rho\|_{\lambda}^{1+\alpha_{0}},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mbox{if}\ 1<p<2,\end{cases}

where
\alpha_{0}
is a positive constant depending on
n,\lambda
and
p.

Remark 9.4
.

We expect that Lemma
9.8
should hold for all
\alpha_{0}<\frac{2-p}{p},
however, we do not know yet how to prove it. Given the Euclidean stability estimate of
[
19
]
,
\alpha_{0}=\frac{2-p}{p}
should be optimal.

10.
Proof of the counter examples

In this section, we prove that
\|\rho\|_{\lambda}\approx\mbox{dist}(u,\mbox{sum of hyperbolic bubbles}),
which will complete the proof of counter-example. First, we need some preparatory lemmas.

Lemma 10.1
.

The followings hold

(a)

For fixed
w\in\mathbb{B}^{n},
the following holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}[z]-\mathcal{U}[w]+2\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(\cdot,w))\frac{\tau_{-w}(\cdot)}{|\tau_{-w}(\cdot)|}\cdot\tau_{-w}(z)=o(|\tau_{-w}(z)|),\ \ \mbox{in}\ H^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})

as
d(z,w)\rightarrow 0.

(b)

\mathcal{U}[w]
is orthogonal to
2\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(\cdot,w))\frac{\tau_{-w}(\cdot)}{|\tau_{-w}(\cdot)|}
with respect to
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda}.

(c)

Moreover, denoting
u_{j}[w]=2\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(\cdot,w))\frac{(\tau_{-w}(\cdot))_{j}}{|\tau_{-w}(\cdot)|},j=1,2,\ldots,n

we have

\displaystyle\|u_{j}[w]\|_{L^{p+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\approx 1,\ \ \ \|u_{j}[w]\|_{\lambda}\approx 1

uniformly with respect to
w,
where
(\tau_{-w}(\cdot))_{j}
denotes the
j
-th component of
\tau_{-w}(\cdot).

Proof.

(a) We first consider the case
w=0.
The general case then follows from this particular case.

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}[z](x)-\mathcal{U}[0](x)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{U}(d(x,z))-\mathcal{U}(d(x,0))

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{ds}\mathcal{U}(d(x,sz))\ ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,sz))\frac{d}{ds}d(x,sz)\ ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,sz))-\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,0))\right)\frac{d}{ds}d(x,sz)\ ds

(10.1)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,0))\left(d(x,z)-d(x,0)\right).

We recall, by definition

(10.2)

\displaystyle V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[0])(x)

\displaystyle=2\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,0))\frac{x_{j}}{|x|}.

Multiplying (
10.2
) by
z_{j}
and summing over
j=1,2,\ldots,n
we get

(10.3)

\displaystyle V[\mathcal{U}[0]](x)\cdot z=2\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,0))\frac{(x\cdot z)}{|x|}

We see from the formula
d(x,sz)=2\ln\left(\frac{\sqrt{1-2sx\cdot z+s^{2}|x|^{2}|z|^{2}}+|x-sz|}{\sqrt{(1-|x|^{2})(1-s^{2}|z|^{2})}}\right)
that

(10.4)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}d(x,sz)=O\left(\frac{|z|}{|x|}\right),\ \ \ \mbox{as}\ |z|\rightarrow 0,

in the
C^{1}
topology and it is easy to see from the same formula that

(10.5)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}d(x,z)|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}=\left(\frac{1-|x|^{2}}{2}\right)|\nabla\ d(x,z)|=O(1),

uniformly in
x
as
z\approx 0.

Now we estimate
d(x,z)-d(x,0)
as
|z|\rightarrow 0.
Using the same formula for the distance we get

\displaystyle e^{d(x,z)-d(x,0)}-1

\displaystyle=\frac{(\sqrt{1-2x\cdot z+|x|^{2}|z|^{2}}+|x-z|)^{2}}{(1+|x|)^{2}(1-|z|^{2})}-1

(10.6)

\displaystyle=\frac{-4x\cdot z-2|x|+2|x-z|\sqrt{1-2x\cdot z+|x|^{2}|z|^{2}}+O(|z|^{2})}{(1+|x|)^{2}(1-|z|^{2})}

Now

(10.7)

\displaystyle 2|x-z|\sqrt{1-2x\cdot z+|x|^{2}|z|^{2}}

\displaystyle=2\sqrt{(|x|^{2}-2x\cdot z+|z|^{2})(1-2x\cdot z+|x|^{2}|z|^{2})}

(10.8)

\displaystyle=2\sqrt{|x|^{2}-2(x\cdot z)(1+|x|^{2})+O(|z|^{2})}

On the other hand

(10.9)

\displaystyle\left(|x|-\frac{x\cdot z}{|x|}(1+|x|^{2})\right)^{2}=|x|^{2}-2(x\cdot z)(1+|x|^{2})+O\left(\frac{|z|^{2}}{|x|}\right),

in the
C^{1}
-topology. Combining (
10
),(
10.7
) and (
10.9
) we get

\displaystyle e^{d(x,z)-d(x,0)}-1

\displaystyle=\frac{-4x\cdot z-2\frac{x\cdot z}{|x|}(1+|x|^{2})+O(|z|^{2})}{(1+|x|)^{2}(1-|z|^{2})}

\displaystyle=-2\frac{\frac{x\cdot z}{|x|}(1+|x|)^{2}+O(|z|^{2})}{(1+|x|)^{2}(1-|z|^{2})}

\displaystyle=-2\frac{x\cdot z}{|x|}+O(|z|^{2}).

As a result, we deduce

(10.10)

\displaystyle d(x,z)-d(x,0)=\ln\left(1-2\frac{x\cdot z}{|x|}+O\left(\frac{|z|^{2}}{|x|}\right)\right)=-2\frac{x\cdot z}{|x|}+O\left(\frac{|z|^{2}}{|x|}\right).

We get from (
10
), (
10.3
) and (
10.10
) that

(10.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}[z](x)-\mathcal{U}[0](x)+V[\mathcal{U}[0]](x)\cdot z

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,sz))-\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,0))\right)\frac{d}{ds}d(x,sz)\ ds

(10.12)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +O\left(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,0))\frac{|z|^{2}}{|x|}\right),

in
H^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n}),
which completes the proof of the lemma for
w=0
.

For general case, thanks to (
10.10
) we note that

\displaystyle d(x,z)-d(x,w)

\displaystyle=d(\tau_{-w}(x),\tau_{-w}(z))-d(\tau_{-w}(x),0)

\displaystyle=-2\frac{\tau_{-w}(x)}{|\tau_{-w}(x)|}\cdot\tau_{-w}(z)+O\left(\frac{|\tau_{-w}(z)|^{2}}{|\tau_{-w}(x)|}\right).

as
|\tau_{-w}(z)|\rightarrow 0,
a consequence of
d(z,w)=d(\tau_{-w}(z),0)=\ln\left(\frac{1+|\tau_{-w}(z)|}{1-|\tau_{-w}(z)|}\right)\rightarrow 0.
Now the proof can be completed as before.

(b) For simplicity we denote
u=2\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(\cdot,w))\frac{(\tau_{-w}(\cdot))_{j}}{|\tau_{-w}(\cdot)|},
for some
j\in\{1,2,\ldots,n\},
where
(\tau_{-w}(\cdot))_{j}
denotes the
j
-th component of
\tau_{-w}(\cdot).
Multiplying the equation satisfied by
\mathcal{U}[w]:

-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\mathcal{U}[w]-\lambda\mathcal{U}[w]=\mathcal{U}[w]^{p}
by
u
and integrating by parts we get

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{U}[w],u\rangle_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\mathcal{U}[w]^{p}u\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\mathcal{U}(d(x,w))^{p}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,w))\frac{(\tau_{-w}(x))_{j}}{|\tau_{-w}(\cdot)|}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\mathcal{U}(d(x,0))^{p}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}d(x,0)\frac{x_{j}}{|x|}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\mathcal{U}[0]^{p}V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[0])\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}=0

where in the third line we used the change of variable formula.

(c) Direct computation using the change of variable formula gives

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}|u_{j}[w](x)|^{p+1}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}

\displaystyle\approx\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\left|\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,w))\frac{(\tau_{-w}(x))_{j}}{|\tau_{-w}(x)|}\right|^{p+1}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\left|\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,0))\frac{x_{j}}{|x|}\right|^{p+1}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\approx\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}|V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[0])|^{p+1}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\approx 1.

The statement regarding the Sobolev norm now follows from the Poincaré-Sobolev inequality and the bound on
|\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}|\lesssim\mathcal{U}.

∎

Remark 10.1
.

The expression of
u_{j}[w]
is slightly different from
V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[w]).
In the appendix section we will derive an explicit expression of
V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[w])
and show that
u_{j}[w]
is a linear combination of
\{V_{k}(\mathcal{U}[w])\}_{k=1}^{n}.

Lemma 10.2
.

There exists a constant
C_{0}
such that for every
R\approx 1
and
\phi\in\mathcal{F},
there holds

\displaystyle C_{0}^{-1}\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq\|\phi\|_{\lambda}\leq C_{0}\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we may assume that
\phi\in F_{U},
i.e.,
\phi
satisfies

\displaystyle-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\phi-\lambda\phi=\mu U^{p-1}\phi,\ \ \ \ \mbox{where}\ \mu=1\ \mbox{or}\ p.

Multiplying the above equation by
\phi
and integration by parts gives

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{\lambda}^{2}=\mu\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\phi^{2}U^{p-1}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\leq\mu\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\phi^{2}(U+V)^{p-1}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}},

which gives one side of the inequality. On the other side

(10.13)

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{\lambda}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\phi^{2}U^{p-1}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\phi^{2}(U+V)^{p-1}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}+\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\phi^{2}(U^{p-1}-(U+V)^{p-1})\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}.

We can estimate

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\phi^{2}(U^{p-1}-(U+V)^{p-1})\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\phi\|_{L^{p+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{2}\|U^{p-1}-(U+V)^{p-1}\|_{L^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{\frac{p-1}{p+1}}

(10.14)

\displaystyle\leq o(1)\|\phi\|_{\lambda}^{2}.

As a result (
10
) and (
10.13
) together give the other side of the inequality, hence completing the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 10.3
.

Let
\rho
be defined as in Lemma
9.3
and
u=U+V+\rho.
If
R

is sufficiently close to
1,
then

\displaystyle\|\rho\|_{\lambda}\lesssim\inf_{c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathbb{R},z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{B}^{n}}\|u-c_{1}\mathcal{U}[z_{1}]-c_{2}\mathcal{U}[z_{2}]\|_{\lambda}

Proof.

We define
\sigma=U+V
and
\sigma^{\prime}=c_{1}\mathcal{U}[z_{1}]+c_{2}\mathcal{U}[z_{2}],
then

u-\sigma^{\prime}=\rho+(\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}).

If
\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda}\geq 2\|\rho\|_{\lambda},
then
\|u-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda}\geq\|\rho\|_{\lambda}
and hence there is nothing to prove. So we may assume
\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda}<2\|\rho\|_{\lambda}.
Since
\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\rightarrow 0
as
R\uparrow 1
we have
\|\rho\|_{\lambda}\rightarrow 0
and hence
\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda}\rightarrow 0
as
R\uparrow 1.
To begin with, we may without loss of generality assume that
\|U-c_{1}\mathcal{U}[z_{1}]\|_{\lambda}=o(1)
and
\|V-c_{2}\mathcal{U}[z_{2}]\|_{\lambda}=o(1).

We define

\displaystyle\delta=|c_{1}-1|+|c_{2}-1|+|\tau_{Re_{1}}(z_{1})|+|\tau_{-Re_{1}}(z_{2})|,

then
\delta=o(1)
as
R\uparrow 1.

We will show that
\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda}\approx\delta.
We compute

\displaystyle\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda}^{2}=\|U-c_{1}\mathcal{U}[z_{1}]\|_{\lambda}^{2}+\|V-c_{2}\mathcal{U}[z_{2}]\|_{\lambda}^{2}+2\langle U-c_{1}\mathcal{U}[z_{1}],V-c_{2}\mathcal{U}[z_{2}]\rangle_{\lambda}.

Note that
d(z_{1},-Re_{1})=d(z_{1},\tau_{-Re_{1}}(0))=d(\tau_{Re_{1}}(z_{1}),0)\approx|\tau_{Re_{1}}(z_{1})|
as

d(z_{1},-Re_{1})\rightarrow 0.
Similarly for
z_{2}.

Since
V_{j}(U)
(respectively,
V_{j}(V)
) is
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda}
orthogonal to
U
(respectively,
V
)
we deduce from Lemma
10.1

\displaystyle\|U-c_{1}\mathcal{U}[z_{1}]\|_{\lambda}^{2}=\|(1-c_{1})U+c_{1}(U-\mathcal{U}[z_{1}])\|_{\lambda}^{2}\approx(|c_{1}-1|+|\tau_{Re_{1}}(z_{1})|)^{2}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\|V-c_{2}\mathcal{U}[z_{2}]\|_{\lambda}^{2}=\|(1-c_{2})U+c_{2}(U-\mathcal{U}[z_{2}])\|_{\lambda}^{2}\approx(|c_{2}-1|+|\tau_{-Re_{1}}(z_{2})|)^{2}.

On the other hand, using again Lemma
10.1
and fact that the interaction between
U
and
V
is small as
R\approx 1
and
|V_{j}(U)|\lesssim U,\ |V_{j}(V)|\lesssim V
we get

(10.15)

\displaystyle\langle U-c_{1}\mathcal{U}[z_{1}],V-c_{2}\mathcal{U}[z_{2}]\rangle_{\lambda}\lesssim o(\delta^{2})+\delta^{2}\|UV\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}=o(\delta^{2}),

and hence

\displaystyle\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda}\approx\delta.

The next step is to show that

(10.16)

\displaystyle|\langle\rho,\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\rangle_{\lambda}|=o(\|\rho\|_{\lambda}\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda})

which would conclude that

\displaystyle\|u-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda}=\|\rho+(\sigma-\sigma^{\prime})\|_{\lambda}\approx\|\rho\|_{\lambda}+\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda}\geq\|\rho\|_{\lambda},

and complete the proof of the lemma. The remains of the proof of the lemma will be concentrated on the
proof of (
10.16
). Let
\rho^{\bot}
be the orthogonal projection of
\rho
on to
\mathcal{F}^{\bot}
with respect to the
L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
inner product. Recall that
\rho\in\mathcal{E}^{\bot}.
Since the subspaces
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{F}
are close and the distance between
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{F}

is the same as the distance between
\mathcal{E}^{\bot}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\bot},
Lemma
9.6
and (
9.23
) imply

(10.17)

\displaystyle\|\rho-\rho^{\bot}\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq d(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})\|\rho\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}=o\left(\|\rho\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\right).

Moreover, from Lemma
9.3
and (
9.6
) we deduce

(10.18)

\displaystyle\|\rho\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{2}\leq\|\rho\|_{\lambda}^{2}+\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\tilde{f}\rho\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\right|\lesssim\|\rho\|_{\lambda}^{2}.

On the other hand, since
\rho-\rho^{\bot}\in\mathcal{F},
Lemma
10.2
gives

(10.19)

\displaystyle\|\rho-\rho^{\bot}\|_{L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\approx\|\rho-\rho^{\bot}\|_{\lambda}.

Hence (
10.17
), (
10.18
), (
10.19
) together gives

(10.20)

\displaystyle\|\rho-\rho^{\bot}\|_{\lambda}=o(\|\rho\|_{\lambda}).

As a corollary of (
10.20
) we deduce

\displaystyle|\langle\sigma-\sigma^{\prime},\rho\rangle_{\lambda}|

\displaystyle\leq|\langle\sigma-\sigma^{\prime},\rho^{\bot}\rangle_{\lambda}|+|\langle\sigma-\sigma^{\prime},\rho-\rho^{\bot}\rangle_{\lambda}|

\displaystyle\leq|\langle\sigma-\sigma^{\prime},\rho^{\bot}\rangle_{\lambda}|+\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda}\|\rho-\rho^{\bot}\|_{\lambda}

(10.21)

\displaystyle\leq|\langle\sigma-\sigma^{\prime},\rho^{\bot}\rangle_{\lambda}|+o(\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda}\|\rho\|_{\lambda}),

In order to estimate
|\langle\sigma-\sigma^{\prime},\rho^{\bot}\rangle_{\lambda}|
we decompose it as

(10.22)

\displaystyle|\langle\sigma-\sigma^{\prime},\rho^{\bot}\rangle_{\lambda}|\leq|\langle U-c_{1}\mathcal{U}[z_{1}],\rho^{\bot}\rangle_{\lambda}|+|\langle V-c_{2}\mathcal{U}[z_{2}],\rho^{\bot}\rangle_{\lambda}|,

and noting that the two terms are identical in nature, it is enough to estimate the first term.

To proceed further, we use the following: Let us denote by
\mathcal{F}^{\bot}
the
L^{2}_{(U+V)^{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
-the orthogonal complement of
\mathcal{F}.
Let
\psi\in\mathcal{F}^{\bot}\cap H^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
and
\phi\in\mathcal{F}.
With out loss of generality assume that
-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\phi-\lambda\phi=\mu U^{p-1}\phi
where
\mu=1
or
p.
Then

\displaystyle\left|\langle\psi,\phi\rangle_{\lambda}\right|=\left|\mu\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\psi\phi U^{p-1}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mu\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\psi\phi(U^{p-1}-(U+V)^{p-1})\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\psi\|_{L^{p+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\|(U^{p-1}-(U+V)^{p-1})\|_{L^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\|\phi\|_{L^{p+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}

(10.23)

\displaystyle\lesssim o(1)\|\psi\|_{\lambda}\|\phi\|_{\lambda},

holds for every
\psi\in\mathcal{F}^{\bot}\cap H^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
and
\phi\in\mathcal{F}.
Since
U,V_{j}(U)\in\mathcal{F}
and
\rho^{\bot}\in\mathcal{F}^{\bot},
using (
10
) we get

\displaystyle|\langle(1-c_{1})U-c_{1}V(U)\cdot\tau_{Re_{1}}(z_{1}),\rho^{\bot}\rangle_{\lambda}|

\displaystyle\lesssim o(1)\|(1-c_{1})U-c_{1}V(U)\cdot\tau_{Re_{1}}(z_{1})\|_{\lambda}\|\rho^{\bot}\|_{\lambda}

\displaystyle\lesssim o(1)(|c_{1}-1|+|\tau_{Re_{1}}(z_{1})|)\|\rho\|_{\lambda}

\displaystyle\lesssim o(1)\delta\|\rho\|_{\lambda}\lesssim o(\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda}\|\rho\|_{\lambda}),

where in second last line we have used
\|\rho^{\bot}\|_{\lambda}\approx\|\rho\|_{\lambda}
(thanks to (
10.20
)). Therefore

\displaystyle|\langle U-c_{1}\mathcal{U}[z_{1}],\rho^{\bot}\rangle_{\lambda}|

\displaystyle\leq|\langle(1-c_{1})U-c_{1}V(U)\cdot\tau_{Re_{1}}(z_{1}),\rho^{\bot}\rangle_{\lambda}|

\displaystyle\ \ +|c_{1}\langle U+V(U)\cdot\tau_{Re_{1}}(z_{1})-\mathcal{U}[z_{1}],\rho^{\bot}\rangle_{\lambda}|

\displaystyle\leq o(\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda}\|\rho\|_{\lambda})+\|U+V(U)\cdot\tau_{Re_{1}}(z_{1})-\mathcal{U}[z_{1}]\|_{\lambda}\|\rho^{\bot}\|_{\lambda}

\displaystyle\leq o(\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda}\|\rho\|_{\lambda})+o(|\tau_{Re_{1}}(z_{1})|)\|\rho\|_{\lambda}

(10.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim o(\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\lambda}\|\rho\|_{\lambda}).

The desired bound (
10.16
) now follows from (
10.20
), (
10
), (
10.22
) and (
10
).
∎

10.1.
The function
u
and
u^{+}
are counter examples

In this section, we will complete the proof of counter-example. First, we prove Theorem
9.1

and then we will show as in
[
28
]
that
u^{+}:=\max\{u,0\}
satisfies the same assertion of Theorem
9.1
. Before starting the proof let us briefly state and prove a lemma which would give us control on
\|u^{-}\|_{\lambda}.

Lemma 10.4
.

There holds

(10.25)

\|u^{-}\|_{\lambda}\lesssim\begin{cases}\|\rho\|_{\lambda}|\ln\|\rho\|_{\lambda}|^{-\frac{1}{4}},\ \ \ \ \mbox{if}\ p=2,\\
\|\rho\|_{\lambda}^{1+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mbox{if}\ 1<p<2,\end{cases}

where
u^{-}=-\mbox{min}\{u,0\}.

Proof.

By applying divergence theorem to the vector field
u^{-}\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u
we deduce

(10.26)

\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}|\nabla_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u^{-}|^{2}\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\cap\{u<0\}}u\,(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u)\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}.

We recall that
u=U+V+\rho,
then
u
satisfies the following equation

-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u\,=\,\lambda u+U^{p}+V^{p}+p(U+V)^{p-1}\rho\,+\,\tilde{f}.

Moreover if
u<0,
then
U+V<|\rho|.
Hence in the region
\{u<0\}
it holds
|u|\lesssim|\rho|
and
f\lesssim|\rho|^{p}.
Hence

\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\cap\{u<0\}}u\,(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u)\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}=\lambda\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\cap\{u<0\}}(u^{-})^{2}\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\,+\,\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\cap\{u<0\}}u(U^{p}+V^{p}+p(U+V)^{p-1}\rho\,+\,\tilde{f})\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}.

Now substituting back in (
10.26
) we obtain

\displaystyle\|u^{-}\|_{\lambda}^{2}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\rho^{p+1}\,+\,\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}|\rho||f-\tilde{f}|\,{\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\lesssim\|\rho\|^{p+1}_{\lambda}\,+\,\|\rho\|_{\lambda}\|f-\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\frac{p+1}{p}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}.

Now the result follows from Lemma
9.7
.

∎

Proof of Theorem
9.1
.

Proof.

Let
u=U+V+\rho
be as defined in (
9.4
) and set
\mathcal{D}=\inf_{c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathbb{R},z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{B}^{n}}\|u-c_{1}\mathcal{U}[z_{1}]-c_{2}\mathcal{U}[z_{2}]\|_{\lambda}.
Note that
u
depends on
R.
Then by (
9.6
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}\leq\|u-U-V\|_{\lambda}=\|\rho\|_{\lambda}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\rightarrow 0,

as
R\uparrow 1.
On the other hand by Lemma
10.3
,
\|\rho\|_{\lambda}\lesssim\mathcal{D}
and hence
\|\rho\|_{\lambda}\approx\mathcal{D}
as
R\uparrow 1.
Using this in Lemma
9.8
we infer that

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u+\lambda u+|u|^{p-1}u\|_{L^{\frac{p+1}{p}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\begin{cases}\mathcal{D}|\ln\mathcal{D}|^{-\frac{1}{2}},\ \ \ \ \mbox{if}\ p=2,\\
\mathcal{D}^{1+\alpha_{0}},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mbox{if}\ 1<p<2,\end{cases}

completing the proof of part
(a)
and part
(b)
of the theorem.

Now we begin the proof for
u^{+},
i.e., part
(c).
We essentially follow the steps as in
[
28
, Section 4.6]
. For the sake of brevity, we shall outline the minor modifications.
We prove that
u^{+}=(u_{R})^{+}
satisfies the requirements. One can see this immediately using Lemma
10.4

\left|\|u^{+}-U-V\|_{\lambda}-\|u-U-V\|_{\lambda}\right|\leq\|u^{-}\|_{\lambda}\lesssim\circ(\|u-U-V\|_{\lambda}).

The rest of the proof follows by realising the following estimates

(10.27)

\|u^{+}-c_{1}\mathcal{U}[z_{1}]-c_{2}\mathcal{U}[z_{2}]\|_{\lambda}\approx\|u-c_{1}\mathcal{U}[z_{1}]-c_{2}\mathcal{U}[z_{2}]\|_{\lambda},

and

(10.28)

\|\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u^{+}+\lambda u^{+}+(u^{+})^{p}\|_{H^{-1}}\lesssim\begin{cases}\|\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u+\lambda u+u|u|^{p-1}\|_{H^{-1}}\,+\,\|\rho\|_{\lambda}|\ln\|\rho\|_{\lambda}|^{-\frac{1}{4}},\ \ \ \ \mbox{if}\ p=2,\\
\|\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u+\lambda u+u|u|^{p-1}\|_{H^{-1}}\,+\,\|\rho\|_{\lambda}^{1+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mbox{if}\ 1<p<2.\end{cases}

The proof of the above estimates follows along the line of
[
28
, Proof of Theorem 4.3]
. Indeed, using triangle inequality we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\|u^{+}-c_{1}\mathcal{U}[z_{1}]-c_{2}\mathcal{U}[z_{2}]\|_{\lambda}-\|u-c_{1}\mathcal{U}[z_{1}]-c_{2}\mathcal{U}[z_{2}]\|_{\lambda}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|u^{+}-u\|_{\lambda}=\|u^{-}\|_{\lambda}

\displaystyle\lesssim\begin{cases}\|\rho\|_{\lambda}|\ln\|\rho\|_{\lambda}|^{-\frac{1}{4}},\ \ \ \ \mbox{if}\ p=2,\\
\|\rho\|_{\lambda}^{1+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mbox{if}\ 1<p<2.\\
\end{cases}

Now the estimate (
10.27
) follows by using Lemma
10.3
and the fact that
\frac{t|\log t|^{-\frac{1}{4}}}{t}\rightarrow 0
and
\frac{t^{1+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}}}{t}\rightarrow 0

when
t\rightarrow 0.
For (
10.28
), using triangle and Sobolev inequalities we deduce

\displaystyle\left|\|\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u^{+}+\lambda u^{+}(u^{+})^{p}\|_{H^{-1}}-\|\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}+\lambda u+u|u|^{p-1}\|\right|_{H^{-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u^{+}-u\|_{\lambda}+\|(u^{+})^{p}-u|u|^{p-1}\|_{H^{-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u^{-}\|_{\lambda}+\|(u^{-})^{p}\|_{L^{\frac{p+1}{p}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u^{-}\|_{\lambda}+\|u^{-}\|^{p}_{\lambda}\lesssim\|u^{-}\|_{\lambda}.

The desired estimate now follows from (
10.27
), (
10.28
) and Lemma
10.4
. This completes the proof of part
(c)
of Theorem
9.1
.
∎

Appendix A
Non-degenaracy and spectral properties of the linearized operator

In this subsection, we collect a few key lemmas needed for the proof related to the eigenvalues and eigenfunctions of the linearized operator

\mathcal{L}:=(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)/\mathcal{U}[z]^{p-1}.

We know that if
\tau_{b}
is a hyperbolic translation then
\mathcal{U}[z]\circ\tau_{b},
also solves (
1.4
) and hence the kernel of the linearized operator contains non-trivial elements. It was shown in
[
36
]
that the degeneracy happens only along an
n
-dimensional subspace characterized by the vector fields

\displaystyle V_{j}(x):=(1+|x|^{2})\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}\,-\,2x_{j}\sum_{l=1}^{n}x_{l}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{l}}.

for
j=1,\ldots,n.
More precisely, we define

\displaystyle\Phi_{j}(x):=\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}\mathcal{U}[z]\circ\tau_{te_{j}},\ \ 1\leq i\leq n,

then
\Phi_{j}(x)=V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[z]),

\Phi_{j}
solves the eigenvalue problem

(A.1)

\displaystyle-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\Phi_{j}-\lambda\Phi_{j}=p\,\mathcal{U}[z]^{p-1}\,\Phi_{j}.

and the degeneracy in the solution space to (
A.1
) can occur only along the directions
\Phi_{j},1\leq j\leq n.

Theorem A

(
[
36
]
)
.

Let
V_{j}
be the vector fields in
\mathbb{B}^{n}
defined above and
\Phi_{j}=V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[z]).
Then
\{\Phi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}
forms a basis for the kernel of
(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda-p\mathcal{U}[z]^{p-1}).

As a result, we obtain complete information on the first and the second eigenvalues and corresponding eigenspaces of the operator
\mathcal{L}.
We recall the following result from our earlier work
[
5
]

Proposition A.1
.

The first and the second eigenvalues of the operator

\mathcal{L}:=(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)/\mathcal{U}[z]^{p-1}

are respectively
1
and
p.
Moreover, the first eigenspace is one dimensional and spanned by
\mathcal{U}[z]
and the second eigenspace is
n
-dimensional and spanned by
\{\Phi_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}.

In this sequel, we also recall another relevant result used in this article whose proof can be found in
[
36
]
.

Lemma A.1
.

The first Dirichlet eigenvalue of the operator

\mathcal{L}:=(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)/\mathcal{U}[z]^{p-1}

in the negative half
\mathbb{B}^{n}_{-}:=\{x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{B}^{n}\ :\ x_{j}<0\}
is
p
for every
j=1,\ldots,n
and the corresponding eigenspace is one dimensional spanned by
V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[z]).

A.1.
Some properties of
V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[z])

The main purpose of this section is to derive an explicit expression of
V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[z])
where
z\in\mathbb{B}^{n}
and
1\leq j\leq n
and to show that several norms of
V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[z])
remains uniformly bounded below and above as
z\rightarrow\infty
in
\mathbb{B}^{n}.
We divide the proof into several small lemmas.

Lemma A.2
.

For every
z\in\mathbb{B}^{n}
and
1\leq j\leq n,

\displaystyle\sinh d(x,z)\ \frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}d(\tau_{te_{j}}(x),z)=\frac{4[x_{j}(1+|z|^{2})-z_{j}(1+|x|^{2})]}{(1-|z|^{2})(1-|x|^{2})},

for all
x\in\mathbb{B}^{n}.

Proof.

Differentiating the hyperbolic translation
\tau_{te_{j}}(x)=\frac{(1-t^{2})x+t(|x|^{2}+2tx_{j}+1)e_{j}}{t^{2}|x|^{2}+2tx_{j}+1}
we get

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}\tau_{te_{j}}(x)=(1+|x|^{2})e_{j}-2x_{j}x.

Again differentiating the
\cosh d(\tau_{te_{j}}(x),z)=1+\frac{2|\tau_{te_{j}}(x)-z|^{2}}{(1-|z|^{2})(1-|\tau_{te_{j}}(x)|^{2})}

we get

\displaystyle\sinh d(x,z)\ \frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}d(\tau_{te_{j}}(x),z)=

\displaystyle\frac{4(x-z)\cdot[(1+|x|^{2})e_{j}-2x_{j}x]}{(1-|z|^{2})(1-|x|^{2})}

\displaystyle+\frac{4|x-z|^{2}}{(1-|z|^{2})(1-|x|^{2})^{2}}\ x\cdot[(1+|x|^{2})e_{j}-2x_{j}x]

\displaystyle=\frac{4[(1+|x|^{2})e_{j}-2x_{j}x]}{(1-|z|^{2})(1-|x|^{2})^{2}}\cdot[(x-z)(1-|x|^{2})+|x-z|^{2}x]

Direct computations give the result.
∎

As a consequence of the above lemma, we have the following

Corollary A.1
.

The explicit expression of
V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[z])
is given by

\displaystyle V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[z])(x)=2\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,z))\frac{x_{j}(1+|z|^{2})-z_{j}(1+|x|^{2})}{|x-z|\sqrt{1-2x\cdot z+|x|^{2}|z|^{2}}},\ \ \mbox{for}\ x\in\mathbb{B}^{n}.

Proof.

By definition

\displaystyle V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[z])(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}\mathcal{U}[z]\circ\tau_{te_{j}}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}\mathcal{U}(d(\tau_{te_{j}}(x),z))

(A.2)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,z))\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}d(\tau_{te_{j}}(x),z).

Again from the explicit expression of the hyperbolic distance

(A.3)

\displaystyle\sinh d(x,z)=2\sinh\frac{d(x,z)}{2}\cosh\frac{d(x,z)}{2}=2\frac{|x-z|\sqrt{1-2x\cdot z+|x|^{2}|z|^{2}}}{(1-|z|^{2})(1-|x|^{2})}.

The lemma now follows from (
A.1
), (
A.3
) and Lemma
A.2
.
∎

Note that
V_{j}
and
u_{j}
from Lemma
10.1
are not the same. Actually,
u_{j}
are linear combinations of
V_{k}
s
as indicated in the next lemma.

Lemma A.3
.

For
j\in\{1,2,\ldots,n\}
we have

\displaystyle u_{j}[z]=\sum_{k=1}^{n}c_{k}V_{k}(\mathcal{U}[z]),

where

\displaystyle c_{k}=\begin{cases}\frac{z_{k}z_{j}}{1+|z|^{2}},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mbox{if}\ k\neq j,\\
\\
\frac{1-|z|^{2}-2z_{j}^{2}}{1+|z|^{2}},\ \ \ \mbox{if}\ k=j.\end{cases}

Proof.

It is not difficult to check from the expression of
\tau_{-z}(x)=\frac{(1-|z|^{2})x-(|x|^{2}-2x\cdot z+1)z}{|z|^{2}|x|^{2}-2x\cdot z+1}
that

\displaystyle|\tau_{-z}(x)|^{2}=\frac{|x-z|^{2}}{|z|^{2}|x|^{2}-2x\cdot z+1}.

Hence

\displaystyle u_{j}[z](x)

\displaystyle=2\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,z))\frac{(\tau_{-z}(x))_{j}}{|\tau_{-z}(x)|}

\displaystyle=2\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,z))\frac{(1-|z|^{2})x_{j}-(|x|^{2}-2x\cdot z+1)z_{j}}{|x-z|\sqrt{1-2x\cdot z+|x|^{2}|z|^{2}}}

\displaystyle=V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[z])+2\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,z))\frac{2((x\cdot z)z_{j}-|z|^{2}x_{j})}{|x-z|\sqrt{1-2x\cdot z+|x|^{2}|z|^{2}}}

Therefore it is enough to express
2((x\cdot z)z_{j}-|z|^{2}x_{j})
in terms of
x_{k}(1+|z|^{2})-z_{k}(1+|x|^{2})
.
We put

\displaystyle 2((x\cdot z)z_{j}-|z|^{2}x_{j})=\sum_{k=1}^{n}d_{k}(x_{k}(1+|z|^{2})-z_{k}(1+|x|^{2})),

which should hold for all
x\in\mathbb{B}^{n}.
Putting
x=0
we derive
\sum_{k=1}^{n}d_{k}z_{k}=0
and hence

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}d_{k}x_{k}(1+|z|^{2})=2((x\cdot z)z_{j}-|z|^{2}x_{j})=2\sum_{k\neq j}z_{k}z_{j}x_{k}+2(z_{j}^{2}-|z|^{2})x_{j}.

Comparing the coefficients we deduce
d_{k}=2z_{k}z_{j}/(1+|z|^{2})
for
k\neq j
and
d_{j}=2(z_{j}^{2}-|z|^{2})/(1+|z|^{2}).

It is now easy to see that
\sum_{k}d_{k}z_{k}=0
is also satisfied. Plugging it in the expression of
u_{j}
we conclude the result.
∎

Though not needed for this article it is interesting to investigate the corresponding results of Lemma
10.1
for

V_{j}
’s. We do it for the particular case
z=Re_{1}.

For
j=2,\ldots,n

(A.4)

\displaystyle\sinh d(x,Re_{1})\ \frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}d(\tau_{te_{j}}(x),Re_{1})=\frac{4[x_{j}(1+R^{2})]}{(1-R^{2})(1-|x|^{2})}

Again from the formula of the hyperbolic distance, we see that

\displaystyle\sinh d(x,Re_{1})

\displaystyle=2\sinh\frac{d(x,Re_{1})}{2}\cosh\frac{d(x,Re_{1})}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2R|x-Re_{1}||x-R^{-1}e_{1}|}{(1-R^{2})(1-|x|^{2})}\lesssim\frac{1}{(1-R^{2})(1-|x|^{2})}.

On the other hand, from (
A.4
), we deduce that on the region
\{x\in\mathbb{B}^{n}\ |\ |x_{j}|\geq\delta\}

for some
\delta>0
small

(A.5)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}d(\tau_{te_{j}}(x),Re_{1})\right|\gtrsim_{\delta}1.

When
j=1,
we have
x_{1}(1+R^{2})-R(1+|x|^{2})=R(x-Re_{1})\cdot(x-R^{-1}e_{1})
and hence

(A.6)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}d(\tau_{te_{1}}(x),Re_{1})=\frac{f_{R}(x)}{\sinh(x,Re_{1})},

where
f_{R}(x)=\frac{4R(x-Re_{1})\cdot(x-R^{-1}e_{1})}{(1-R^{2})(1-|x|^{2})}.
In either case it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}d(\tau_{te_{j}}(x),Re_{1})\right|\lesssim 1,\ \ \mbox{for all}\ j=1,2,\ldots,n.

Now we find an explicit expression of
V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[Re_{1}]).
By definition
\mathcal{U}[Re_{1}](x)=\mathcal{U}\circ\tau_{-Re_{1}}(x)
and hence
\mathcal{U}[Re_{1}]\circ\tau_{te_{j}}(x)=\mathcal{U}(d(\tau_{-Re_{1}}(\tau_{te_{j}}(x)),0))=\mathcal{U}(d(\tau_{te_{j}},Re_{1})).
Hence

\displaystyle V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[Re_{1}])=\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}\mathcal{U}(d(\tau_{te_{j}},Re_{1}))=\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,Re_{1}))\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}d(\tau_{te_{j}}(x),Re_{1}),

which together with
|\mathcal{U}^{\prime}|\lesssim\mathcal{U}
gives

(A.7)

\displaystyle|V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[Re_{1}])|\lesssim\mathcal{U}[Re_{1}],\ \ \mbox{for all}\ j=1,2,\ldots,n.

With this preparation, we can now state the main result on
V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[Re_{1}]).

Lemma A.4
.

For every
1\leq j\leq n
and
R\sim 1

\displaystyle\|V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[Re_{1}])\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\approx 1,\ \ \|V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[Re_{1}])\|_{\lambda}\approx 1.

Proof.

The upper bound follows easily from the equation satisfied by
\Phi_{j}:=V_{j}(\mathcal{U}[Re_{1}])
and (
A.7
). Indeed, multiplying (
A.1
) by
\Phi_{j}
,

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{j}\|_{\lambda}^{2}=p\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\Phi_{j}^{2}\mathcal{U}[Re_{1}]^{p-1}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\mathcal{U}[Re_{1}]^{p+1}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\lesssim 1,

and Poincaré inequality gives the required
L^{2}
-bound. For the lower bound when
j>1
we use (
A.5
) to get

\displaystyle|\Phi_{j}(x)|\gtrsim_{\delta}|\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,Re_{1}))|,\ \ \ \mbox{on}\ \ |x_{j}|\geq\delta.

We fix
R_{0}
such that the hyperbolic ball
B(Re_{1},R_{0})
is entirely contained in the region
|x_{j}|\geq\delta.
Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}|\Phi_{j}(x)|^{2}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\gtrsim_{\delta}\int_{B(Re_{1},R_{0})}|\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,Re_{1}))|^{2}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}=\int_{B(0,R_{0})}|\mathcal{U}^{\prime}|^{2}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\approx 1.

Now for
j=1,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}|\Phi_{1}(x)|^{2}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(x,Re_{1})))^{2}\frac{f_{R}(x)^{2}}{\sinh^{2}d(x,Re_{1})}\ dvg

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(y,0)))^{2}\frac{f_{R}(\tau_{Re_{1}}(y))^{2}}{\sinh^{2}d(y,0)},

where we have used the change of variable
x=\tau_{Re_{1}}(y).
Let us denote
D=R^{2}|x|^{2}+2Ry_{1}+1.
A few straightforward computations give

\displaystyle\tau_{Re_{1}}(y)-Re_{1}

\displaystyle=(1-R^{2})\ \frac{y+R|y|^{2}e_{1}}{D}

\displaystyle\tau_{Re_{1}}(y)-R^{-1}e_{1}

\displaystyle=(1-R^{2})\ \frac{y-2y_{1}e_{1}-R^{-1}e_{1}}{D}

\displaystyle 1-|\tau_{Re_{1}}(y)|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-R^{2})(1-|y|^{2})}{D}.

Hence from the definition of
f_{R}
, we get

\displaystyle f_{R}(\tau_{Re_{1}}(y))

\displaystyle=\frac{4R(\tau_{Re_{1}}(y)-Re_{1})\cdot(\tau_{Re_{1}}(y)-R^{-1}e_{1})}{(1-R^{2})(1-|\tau_{Re_{1}}(y)|^{2})}

\displaystyle=4R\frac{(y+R|y|^{2}e_{1})\cdot(y-2y_{1}e_{1}-e_{1})}{D(1-|y|^{2})}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\frac{4(y+|y|^{2}e_{1})\cdot(y-2y_{1}e_{1}-e_{1})}{|y+e_{1}|^{2}(1-|y|^{2})}=\frac{4g(y)}{(1-|y|^{2})},\ \ \mbox{as}\ \ R\uparrow 1.

Clearly,
g
is bounded above. Recalling
\sinh d(y,0)=\frac{2|y|}{(1-|y|^{2})}
we get

\displaystyle\liminf_{R\rightarrow 1}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}|\Phi_{1}(x)|^{2}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\geq\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(d(y,0)))^{2}\frac{g(y)^{2}}{|y|^{2}}\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\approx 1,

which completes the proof.
∎

A.2.
Spectral properties of weighted Laplace-Beltrami operator

In this subsection, we recall some well-known results on the spectral properties of
(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)/w.
The basic example of weights we consider is one of
U^{p-1},V^{p-1},(U+V)^{p-1}.
Hence we assume

(A.8)

\displaystyle 0<w\in L^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\ \ \mbox{and the embedding}\ H^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\hookrightarrow L^{2}_{w}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\ \ \mbox{is compact}.

As a result,
\left(\frac{-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda}{w}\right)^{-1}:L^{2}_{w}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\to L^{2}_{w}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
is a compact, self-adjoint and positive operator. By standard spectral theory,
L^{2}_{w}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
admits a countable orthonormal basis
\{\psi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
consisting of eigenvectors of
\left(\frac{-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda}{w}\right)^{-1}.
Up to a rearrangement of indices we can write

\displaystyle-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\psi_{k}-\lambda\psi_{k}=\mu_{k}w\psi_{k},\ \ 0<\mu_{1}<\mu_{2}\leq\mu_{3}\leq\cdots\leq\mu_{k}\rightarrow\infty,\ as\ k\rightarrow\infty.

Moreover
\{\mu_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\psi_{k}\}
forms an orthonormal basis for
H^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
with respect to the bilinear form
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda}.

The following theorem is a straightforward adaptation of
[
28
, Lemma A.4 and Lemma A.5]
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
n\geq 3
and
w
be a weight satisfying (
A.8
). Let
\psi\in L^{2}_{w}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
satisfies
-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\psi-\lambda\psi-\mu w\psi=f.
If we write
\psi=\sum_{k}\alpha_{k}\psi_{k}
then

(a)

\sum_{k}\alpha_{k}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\mu}{\lambda_{k}}\right)^{2}=\|(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)^{-1}f\|_{L^{2}_{w}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{2}.

(b)

Moreover, if we assume
\lambda_{k}\geq\frac{\mu}{1-\epsilon},
whenever
\alpha_{k}\neq 0,
where
\epsilon\in(0,1)
then

\displaystyle\|\psi\|_{\lambda}\approx_{\epsilon}\|f\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}.

Proof.

Plugging
\psi=\sum_{k}\alpha_{k}\psi_{k}
in to
-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\psi-\lambda\psi-\mu w\psi=f
we get

\displaystyle\frac{f}{w}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{w}\sum_{k}\alpha_{k}(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\psi_{k}-\lambda\psi_{k}-\mu w\psi_{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{w}\sum_{k}\alpha_{k}(\lambda_{k}w\psi_{k}-\mu w\psi_{k})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k}\alpha_{k}(\lambda_{k}-\mu_{k})\psi_{k}.

Appling
\left(\frac{-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda}{w}\right)^{-1}
both sides and using
\left(\frac{-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda}{w}\right)^{-1}(\psi_{k})=\frac{\psi_{k}}{\lambda_{k}}
we obtain

(A.9)

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\alpha_{k}\left(1-\frac{\mu}{\lambda_{k}}\right)\psi_{k}=(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)^{-1}f.

Since
\{\psi_{k}\}
forms an orthonormal basis for
L^{2}_{w}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
, taking
L^{2}_{w}
-norm on both sides of (
A.9
)
we get
(a).

For (b), we recall that
\{\lambda_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\psi_{k}\}
forms an orthonormal basis of
H^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
with respect to
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda},
and hence

(A.10)

\displaystyle\|\psi\|_{\lambda}^{2}=\sum_{k}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}.

On the other hand, taking the
\|\cdot\|_{\lambda}
-norm on both sides of (
A.9
) we get

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\alpha_{k}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\mu}{\lambda_{k}}\right)^{2}\lambda_{k}\ =\ \|(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)^{-1}f\|_{\lambda}^{2}\ \approx\ \|f\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{2}.

As a result, we get
\|\psi\|_{\lambda}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}.
To prove the equivalence
\approx
in last line we set
v=(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)^{-1}f,
then
(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)v=f
and hence

\|v\|_{\lambda}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}fv\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\leq\|f\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\|v\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\|v\|_{\lambda},

which gives one side of the equivalence. For the other side, for every
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n})

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}f\varphi\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\right|=|\langle v,\varphi\rangle_{\lambda}|\leq\|v\|_{\lambda}\|\varphi\|_{\lambda}\lesssim\|v\|_{\lambda}\|\varphi\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}.

Taking supremum over all
\|\varphi\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq 1
proves the equivalence.

Now if
\lambda_{k}\geq\mu(1-\epsilon)^{-1}
whenever
\alpha_{k}\neq 0,
then
1-\frac{\mu}{\lambda_{k}}\geq\epsilon
and hence

\displaystyle\|f\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{2}\approx\sum_{k}\alpha_{k}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\mu}{\lambda_{k}}\right)^{2}\lambda_{k}\geq\epsilon^{2}\sum_{k}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}^{2}=\epsilon^{2}\|\psi\|_{\lambda}^{2}.

∎

We also used the following simple lemma.

Lemma A.6
.

Let
w
be a weight satisfying (
A.8
) (but not necessarily bounded), then
(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)^{-1}:L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\to L^{2}_{w}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
is a bounded operator.

Proof.

For every
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n}),
we have

\displaystyle\|(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}=1}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)u\varphi\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\|u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)u\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|u\|_{\lambda}^{2}}{\|u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\|u\|_{\lambda}\gtrsim\|u\|_{L^{p+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})},

where in the second last line we have used Poincaré inequality. Since
w\in L^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}u^{2}w\ {\rm d}v_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\leq\|u\|_{L^{p+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{2}\|w\|_{L^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{2}

and hence
\|(-\Delta_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}-\lambda)^{-1}u\|_{L^{2}_{w}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}.

∎
